Closes a bypass where an agent restricted or budget-exhausted in-process could keep acting by delegating to a runtime adapter (aider, claude_code, goose, hermes, …). The runtime dispatch seam charges and checks the same per-session budget and records an audit row for every allow and block; a stable per-task session key ensures a task's dispatch attempts share one budget. Approvals remain fail-closed — a TTL-expired request denies by default and a process restart expires everything pending — now pinned by tests. Verified by: risky-module-review skill (no security check weakened; `_local_safety_check` and workspace path validation untouched; changes add enforcement only).


### Added

- **TokenIn free frontier-model gateway added as a rotatable free brain provider** (2026-08-22). [tokenin.my.id](https://tokenin.my.id/dashboard/models) is a free, OpenAI-compatible gateway (`https://tokenin.my.id/v1/chat/completions`) fronting a rotating pool of frontier models — GPT-5.6, Claude Opus 4.8, Gemini 3.5 Flash, GLM 5.3, DeepSeek V4 Pro, Grok 4.6, Kimi K3, MiniMax M3, Qwen 3.8 Max, Mimo V2.5 — every model `$0` with a small per-model RPM cap (2–7 req/min). It is registered as a new `tokenin` provider (id in the `BrainProvider` Literal), classified **free** so the brain uses it before any paid escalation, and its ten `myt/`-prefixed models become the failover chain: on a per-model `429` the rotation walks to the next model (highest-RPM first; opus kept last so its scarce 2 req/min budget isn't burned first). A `claude-opus-4-8` request now aliases onto tokenin's free `myt/claude-opus-4-8-free`. Config is env-driven: `TOKENIN_API_KEY` (presence adds it, priority 22), `TOKENIN_BASE_URL`, `TOKENIN_MODEL`, and opt-in `TOKENIN_KEY_ROTATION`. Files: `config/models.yaml`, `packages/ai/brain_config.py`, `services/brain_failover.py`, `packages/ai/router.py`, `docs/configuration-reference.md`, `.env.example`, `tests/test_tokenin_provider.py`.
- **Claude 5 family added to the YAML model catalog; Anthropic default promoted to Sonnet 5** (2026-08-21). `claude-sonnet-5`, `claude-sonnet-5-20260501`, `claude-opus-5`, and `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` are now entries in `config/llm/models.yaml` — the YAML model catalog that drives capability filtering, context-window checks, and pricing for all routed requests. Previously these models were registered only in `router/registry.py` (from 2026-08-16), so the YAML-driven code paths (context-window pruning, tool-call routing, cost tracking) could not see them. `claude-sonnet-5` is listed with its confirmed 1M-token context window and introductory pricing ($2/$10 per MTok, kept through September 2026 — cheaper than Sonnet 4.6 at $3/$15). The Anthropic provider default in `config/llm/providers.yaml` is promoted from `claude-sonnet-4-6` to `claude-sonnet-5`; the env-var fallback in `packages/llm/config.py` moves from `claude-sonnet-4-5` to `claude-sonnet-5` for consistency. Files: `config/llm/models.yaml`, `config/llm/providers.yaml`, `packages/llm/config.py`, `tests/test_daily_automation_2026_08_21.py`.
- **`anthropic-workspace-id` response header captured for observability** (2026-08-21). The Anthropic API now returns an `anthropic-workspace-id` header on every response to identify the workspace behind the API key. The Anthropic provider (`packages/llm/providers/anthropic.py`) now reads this header from each chat response, emits it at DEBUG level, and surfaces it in `LLMResponse.raw["_anthropic_workspace_id"]` for downstream observability consumers (Langfuse, the cost tracker, any code that inspects `raw`). Multi-workspace deployments can use this to verify which workspace is serving each request. Files: `packages/llm/providers/anthropic.py`, `tests/test_daily_automation_2026_08_21.py`.
- **Agents can drive a real browser, with a low-RAM Browserbase path** (2026-08-21). Agents already had zero-key internet *reading* (`fetch_url`, `web_search`, `fetch_rss`, `youtube_transcript`); they now also get an interactive browser via a new `browse_page` capability tool (`agent/browser.py`), so they can read JavaScript-rendered pages and verify a deployed UI instead of inferring it from raw HTML. Browser automation is off by default (`BROWSER_AUTOMATION_ENABLED`). When `BROWSERBASE_API_KEY` is set the browser runs in Browserbase's cloud over CDP — the backend never launches a local Chromium (~400MB resident) and needs no browser binaries in its image; without a key it falls back to a local headless Chromium. Navigation is SSRF-screened through `agent/web_reach.unsafe_target_reason` before every request and re-checked after redirects (rule 14), and the guard fails closed. On **Providers → MCP** the `playwright` row now tells the truth: a reachable `PLAYWRIGHT_MCP_URL` shows `connected`, an enabled Browserbase setup shows a green `available` (browser served in-process), and neither shows `unconfigured` with a reason naming both fixes. Files: `agent/browser.py`, `agent/capability_registry.py`, `agent/prompts.py`, `packages/config/settings.py`, `packages/config/control_catalogue.py`, `packages/integrations/mcp_registry.py`, `tests/test_browser.py`, `tests/test_web_reach.py`, `tests/test_mcp_registry.py`, `docs/configuration-reference.md`, `.env.example`.
- **`scripts/refresh_agent_built_proof.py` + monthly `agent-built-proof-refresh` workflow** (2026-08-16). Root-cause fix for the "agent-built proof" numbers: `proof/agent-built.md`, `proof/README.md`, and `README.md` cited merged-PR counts that were hand-typed and had gone stale for five weeks (last updated 2026-07-10; the real counts had moved from 642/176/8 to 829/271/8 by 2026-08-16, and `README.md`'s "most merged PRs here are agent-authored" claim — implying a majority — was false at the true ~34% share). The new script re-runs the same three GitHub search queries the docs already cite (`is:pr is:merged`, `+ head:claude/`, `+ head:codex/`) and rewrites the table, "As of" date, and summary sentence in place, only when the live counts differ from what's committed (`--check` exits 1 on drift without writing, for CI use). The new `.github/workflows/agent-built-proof-refresh.yml` runs it monthly and opens a PR only when the numbers actually moved. 12 new tests in `tests/test_refresh_agent_built_proof.py` cover extraction, rewriting, idempotency against the live docs, and the drift/no-drift `main()` paths. Files: `scripts/refresh_agent_built_proof.py`, `tests/test_refresh_agent_built_proof.py`, `.github/workflows/agent-built-proof-refresh.yml`, `loops/registry.yaml`.
- **Claude Sonnet 5 and Opus 5 registered in the model router** (2026-08-16). `claude-sonnet-5` (1M context, adaptive thinking, cost tier 2) and `claude-opus-5` (flagship, cost tier 3) are now first-class entries in `router/registry.py` and `router/model_router.py`'s built-in alias map, alongside the dated alias `claude-sonnet-5-20260501`. Any client routing through the proxy by those model names is now resolved correctly rather than falling through to the generic heuristic. Files: `router/registry.py`, `router/model_router.py`, `tests/test_model_router.py`.
- **Claude Haiku 4.5 registered in the model router** (2026-08-21). `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` was already routed via `router/model_router.py`'s built-in alias map but had no `router/registry.py` capability profile, so the router couldn't do capability-based selection or fallback for it; the unversioned `claude-haiku-4-5` alias was missing from the map entirely. Both are now registered (`type="coder"`, `cost_tier=1`, `fast` tag) and routable. Files: `router/registry.py`, `router/model_router.py`, `tests/test_model_router.py`.

- **`reasoning_budget` parameter for extended-thinking models** (2026-08-16). The OpenAI-compat `/v1/chat/completions` endpoint now accepts a non-standard `reasoning_budget: "low"|"medium"|"high"|"max"` field alongside the request body. The proxy maps it to the `thinking_token_budget` integer (512 / 2048 / 8192 / 32768) understood by vLLM, Ollama, and the Anthropic passthrough — controlling reasoning depth and cost on DeepSeek-R1, Qwen3, Nemotron, and Claude 3.7+ — then strips the field before forwarding. An existing `thinking_token_budget` in the body is left unchanged. Files: `chat_handlers.py`, `tests/test_daily_automation_2026_08_16.py`.

- **Context pruner wired into the OpenAI-compat chat path** (2026-08-16). `ContextPruner` (already active in the agent loop since 2026-05) now also runs on direct `/v1/chat/completions` requests. Long sessions that exceed ~80k tokens are automatically pruned (3-phase: strip `<think>` tags → backward-walk with per-role budgets → XML-wrap evicted history) before being forwarded to the backend — preventing context-over-limit errors and runaway token spend on multi-turn conversations. Controlled by `PROXY_CONTEXT_PRUNING_ENABLED` (default `true`). Files: `chat_handlers.py`.

- **Telegram Approve→auto-merge gate for green PRs** (2026-08-15). The autonomous agency opens PRs continuously and every one edits `CHANGELOG.md` at the same spot, so each merge re-conflicts the rest — draining the queue by hand is a treadmill. New opt-in sweep (`services/pr_approval_gate.py`, `PR_APPROVAL_GATE_ENABLED`, default off) finds every open non-draft PR whose CI has gone green and posts a one-tap **✅ Approve & merge / ❌ Dismiss** Telegram card. Approve calls the existing service-token endpoint `POST /admin/api/prs/{n}/merge` with a new `auto_merge` mode that enables GitHub **auto-merge** (squash) via GraphQL — GitHub rebases-if-behind and lands the PR when checks pass (using the repo merge queue if configured), so the conflict treadmill resolves itself with no human rebasing. Decision logic (`pr_is_green`, `select_notifiable`) is pure and unit-tested; the sweep is fail-soft and deduped on PR head SHA. Files: `services/pr_approval_gate.py`, `backend/server.py` (`merge_pr_route` auto-merge branch), `telegram_bot.py` (`pr_merge`/`pr_reject` callbacks), `services/background.py`, `docs/configuration-reference.md`, `.env.example`, `tests/test_pr_approval_gate.py`.
- **Anthropic tool-list prompt caching (C6)** (2026-08-15). `AnthropicProvider.build_payload` now marks the last entry of the `tools` array with `cache_control: {type: ephemeral}` when `prompt_caching` is enabled (the default), so Anthropic caches the full tool list up to that breakpoint — ~50–80% fewer tool-definition tokens on repeated agent calls where the tool list is unchanged. The last tool is shallow-copied before annotation so the caller's dicts are never mutated; disable via `ANTHROPIC_PROMPT_CACHING=off`. Salvaged from PR #1230, whose other half (a `search_replace_file` tool) was dropped as a duplicate of the existing `WorkspaceTools.edit_file` (F1, already on master). Files: `packages/llm/providers/anthropic.py`, `tests/test_anthropic_tool_list_caching.py`.
- **Dashboard "Agent Tasks" panel** (2026-08-15). `DashboardHome` now summarises the durable task board — per-status counts (Running / Needs input / Blocked / In review / Failed / Completed / To do) plus the most recent task rows — using the existing `getTaskCounts` / `listTasks` APIs, so the home dashboard answers "what are the agents working on / stuck on / done with" at a glance. Extracted into a reusable presentational `AgentTaskStatus` component with a single `TASK_STATUS_META` vocabulary (mirroring `tasks/models.py::TaskStatus`), rather than a second live feed alongside `AgentActivityFeed`. Best-effort: a task-API failure hides only this section. Files: `frontend/src/components/AgentTaskStatus.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/DashboardHome.js`, `frontend/src/__tests__/agentTaskStatus.test.jsx`.

### Changed

- **Call-volume throttle: the CEO cycle interval is now a Platform Controls setting, defaulting to a calmer 15-minute cadence** (2026-08-15). The CEO loop (`AGENCY_TICK_MINUTES`) assesses state and materialises work on every tick — the single biggest driver of LLM call volume, since each task it creates then plans. On a rate-limited/slow free-tier brain the old 5-minute cadence self-inflicts the provider serialization behind the `planning: TimeoutError` failures. The interval is now a numeric control in **Platform Controls → Autonomy Loops** (adjustable 1–1440 min, takes effect on restart), and the code default is raised 5 → 15 so the agency runs calmer out of the box; lower it once paid LLM capacity is available. Files: `packages/config/control_catalogue.py`, `agent/agency.py`, `docs/configuration-reference.md`, `tests/test_platform_controls.py`.

- **"To be approved" board column replaces the slow approval banner** (2026-08-15). Tasks parked at the pre-execution gate were shown in a full-width banner table above the board that was slow to scan and act on. They now render as a dedicated first Kanban lane ("To be approved"), one card per task with an inline **Approve** button and an optional **comment box** — leave guidance (carried into the run) or approve for the task to be picked up, with a subtle reject. UI only; reuses the existing `awaiting-approval`, `approve-execution`, and comment APIs. Files: `frontend/src/v5/screens/TaskBoardScreen.jsx`.

- **Deep multi-agent swarm and external agent runtimes promoted from beta to stable** (2026-08-15). Both features already carried real, test-covered implementations behind a `beta` maturity flag; they are now `stable` in `features/matrix.py` (the single source of truth), with the docs updated to match. `multi_agent_swarm`: wired into the golden path via `services/ceo_dispatcher.py:CEODispatcher.delegate` — the `WorkflowOrchestrator` EXECUTE phase fans the CEO out across N specialists for medium/high-complexity tasks, with per-subtask cross-verification and a durable CEO ledger (`services/ceo_ledger.py`) for supervision/retry; covered by `tests/test_ceo_dispatcher.py`, `tests/test_multi_agent_swarm.py`, `tests/test_swarm_concurrency.py`, `tests/test_ceo_cross_verify.py`. `sidecar_runtimes` (Hermes/OpenCode/Goose external agent runtimes): every CEO delegation calls `RuntimeManager.wake_all_sleeping_runtimes()` (rate-limited parallel health probes) before dispatch and routes around any still-sleeping sidecar via the fallback chain; covered by `tests/test_runtimes.py`, `tests/test_prime_agent_runtime.py`, `tests/test_runtime_governance.py`. Re-flag either as beta with `FEATURE_MULTI_AGENT_SWARM=beta` / `FEATURE_SIDECAR_RUNTIMES=beta`. Files: `features/matrix.py`, `tests/test_ceo_dispatcher.py`, `docs/architecture/feature-maturity-matrix.md`, `docs/support-matrix.md`, `docs/platform-guide.md`.
- **Removed three dead duplicate UI components** (2026-08-15). `AgentActivityFeed.tsx`, `AgentStatusPanel.tsx`, and `ToolCallViewer.tsx` were unused `.tsx` copies of the `.jsx` components actually imported (this is a Create-React-App / JS project) — 585 lines of silent drift no build ever bundled. Deleted; the imported `.jsx` versions are untouched.

### Fixed

- **A sixth model catalogue was silently re-adding the dead ids** (2026-08-28). `packages/llm/registry.py::_seed_from_legacy` imports `packages/ai/registry.py` and adds any model absent from the YAML catalogues, so removing a retired id from `config/llm/models.yaml` did nothing — `z-ai/glm-5.2`, `meta/llama-4-maverick-17b-128e-instruct` and `meta/llama-3.3-70b-instruct` came straight back, and Maverick was still being offered as a **tool-calling** candidate on the gateway path. Found only by building the registry and asking what it would route to; the removal read as correct and was inert. The legacy registry now carries the three probed-live ids instead.

  `config/llm/models.yaml` gains entries for those three. This matters because `register_discovered` gives any undeclared model `supports_tools: false` and `candidates()` filters tool-calling requests on it — so the new platform default was being dropped from every tool-calling request. `supports_tools` is declared from evidence: the probe gained `--tools`, which sends a real tool definition, and all three returned `tool_calls[get_weather]`. `context_window` and `max_output_tokens` are deliberately conservative floors, not specs: NVIDIA's `/v1/models` returns only `id`/`object`/`created`/`owned_by`, so the real limits cannot be read from the provider and are not invented here. Files: `packages/ai/registry.py`, `config/llm/models.yaml`, `.github/scripts/probe_catalogues.py`, `.github/workflows/catalogue-probe.yml`, `tests/test_one_model_catalogue.py`, `.claude/state/NEXT_ACTION.md`.
- **The agency in GitHub Actions had no Cerebras and no Groq** (2026-08-28). `CLAUDE.md` §4 documents the chain as Cerebras → Groq → NVIDIA NIM → Ollama, and `autonomous-agent.yml` / `autonomous-fix.yml` pass all three keys. Three later workflows — `process-quick-note.yml` (the one that implements issues), `bulk-issue-context.yml` and `issue-context-generator.yml` — passed `NVIDIA_API_KEY` alone, so the agency there had no first or second link at all. A chain missing two links looks exactly like a healthy one until the third dies, which is what happened. All three now pass the full chain. `render.yaml` separately declared no `CEREBRAS_API_KEY` at all — the key set in the dashboard was invisible to this repo and would vanish on a rebuild from the file; it is now declared `sync: false`, which keeps the existing value. `tests/test_agency_workflows_carry_the_failover_chain.py` selects workflows by whether they invoke an agent script rather than by name, so a workflow added later is covered by the same rule. Files: `.github/workflows/process-quick-note.yml`, `.github/workflows/bulk-issue-context.yml`, `.github/workflows/issue-context-generator.yml`, `render.yaml`, `tests/test_agency_workflows_carry_the_failover_chain.py`.
- **Production shipped the dead model regardless of the code — `render.yaml` pinned it in five env values** (2026-08-28). `NVIDIA_DEFAULT_MODEL` and the `AGENT_*_MODEL` variables were literal `value:` entries (not `sync: false`), so they are deployed and override every default in the codebase. Fixing the Python and the catalogues left production still asking for a model that answers 410. All five now carry the probed-live id, and `tests/test_brain_default_consistency.py` fails if `render.yaml` ships any known-retired id or drifts from the catalogue default.

  That test is a rewrite of `tests/test_glm52_brain.py`, which asserted "z-ai/glm-5.2 is the brain" across five surfaces and so pinned a dead model into place — its filename asserted it too. The durable property was never *which* id but that all five surfaces name the **same** one; it now reads the catalogue and checks consistency, and caught `packages/ai/brain.py` still holding its own copy. Six other test modules had the same shape — `test_brain_default_model.py` carried a `LIVE_MODELS` set "live-verified 2026-06-20" whose two entries both answer 410 today, and three tests in `test_unit7_catalog_propagation.py` named *returns catalog preset* and *is not stale* asserted against hardcoded copies of that catalogue. All now derive from the catalogue, so they test the propagation they are named for rather than a frozen copy of its output. Files: `render.yaml`, `packages/ai/brain.py`, `tests/test_brain_default_consistency.py`, `tests/test_brain_default_model.py`, `tests/test_unit7_catalog_propagation.py`, `tests/test_unit8_model_catalog.py`, `tests/test_brain_config_api.py`, `tests/test_brain_config_store.py`.
- **The entire NVIDIA rotation was dead — every candidate, including the replacement** (2026-08-28). The catalogue probe called each id in `config/models.yaml` against the production key. Every one failed: `z-ai/glm-5.2` (410 — the default brain for all four agent roles), `z-ai/glm-5.1` (410), `meta/llama-4-maverick-17b-128e-instruct` (410), `nvidia/llama-3.1-nemotron-70b-instruct` (404), `deepseek-ai/deepseek-r1` (404) — **and `nvidia/nemotron-3-ultra-550b-a55b` (404)**, which the previous entry had just installed as the platform default on the strength of a catalogue listing. A rotation in which every entry is dead does not degrade gracefully; it walks the whole list and reports "All candidate models exhausted", which is verbatim the outage this series began with.

  The rotation is now built only from ids that returned HTTP 200 to a real completion: `nvidia/nemotron-3-super-120b-a12b` (answered with text), `mistralai/mistral-nemotron` (answered `ok`), and `openai/gpt-oss-120b` / `openai/gpt-oss-20b` (200 with an empty body under an 8-token cap, so they sit behind as fallbacks). The super is the new default everywhere the NVIDIA default is decided, and both `config/models.yaml` and the `brain_config.py` fallback copy carry the dead ids in a comment so nothing re-adds them without a probe. `probe_catalogues.py` gained `--model`, which is what made any of this checkable: previously it could only call a provider's default, so a candidate could not be proven before being promoted. Files: `config/models.yaml`, `packages/ai/brain_config.py`, `packages/ai/router.py`, `packages/config/settings.py`, `packages/llm/config.py`, `config/llm/providers.yaml`, `agent/loop.py`, `backend/server.py`, `webui/providers.py`, `setup/api.py`, `runtimes/adapters/internal_agent.py`, `.github/scripts/nvidia_models.py`, `.github/scripts/probe_catalogues.py`, `.github/workflows/catalogue-probe.yml`, `tests/test_nvidia_default_model.py`, `tests/test_brain_failover.py`, `tests/test_provider_router.py`.
- **A dead model in one gateway took the whole provider offline for ten minutes** (2026-08-28). `services/openclaw_gateway.py` hardcoded `z-ai/glm-5.2` and asked every provider for it, so each call drew a 410 — then passed the status to `record_failure`, which opens the provider's circuit for 600s. `get_failover_manager()` is a process-wide singleton, so one dead model there removed NVIDIA from every other caller, including `failover_client`, which deliberately skips just the dead model and tries the next. The gateway now names no model (each provider serves its catalogue default) and a 410 records an ordinary failure instead of cooling the provider. Files: `services/openclaw_gateway.py`.
- **The catalogue probe could not run at all — its first dispatch died on a missing transitive dependency** (2026-08-28). Run 33167878082 failed in ten seconds with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'httpx'`. The probe itself only uses `urllib`, so the workflow installed `pyyaml` alone; but reading the provider config imports `packages.llm.config`, and `packages/llm/__init__` imports the router, which imports `httpx`. The dependency is real and invisible at the call site. The install step now covers both, with a test asserting it — a probe that cannot start is worth catching in the suite rather than on dispatch. Files: `.github/workflows/catalogue-probe.yml`, `tests/test_catalogue_probe.py`.
- **A retired model was still live in the NVIDIA rotation — the earlier fix edited the copy that loses** (2026-08-28). `packages/ai/brain_config.py` carries a hardcoded `PROVIDER_CANDIDATES`, and `config/models.yaml` overwrites it at import. Removing `meta/llama-3.3-70b-instruct` from the Python literal therefore changed nothing: the catalogue still listed it, so it was still tried on every pass through the rotation. Fixed in the catalogue that wins, which also puts `nvidia/nemotron-3-ultra-550b-a55b` and `nvidia/nemotron-3-super-120b-a12b` into the rotation, and the Python copy is reconciled to match. The retired entry is also removed from `config/llm/models.yaml`, where it was an offerable routing option that could only fail.

  The deeper problem this exposed: four files in this repo each declare themselves authoritative for models — `config/models.yaml` ("the single source of truth"), `config/llm/providers.yaml` ("Adding a provider is an entry here. No Python changes"), `config/llm/models.yaml` ("entries here always win") and the `brain_config.py` copy ("kept in sync with this file"). Diffing the last two found **11 of 17 providers disagreeing**; `_MODEL_ALIASES` in `services/brain_failover.py` is a fifth copy. `tests/test_one_model_catalogue.py` does not attempt the consolidation — it stops the bleeding: the divergence set is frozen and a tenth entry fails, the imported rotation must equal the catalogue rather than the literal, and retired ids are checked against what wins at import, which is the check that would have caught the cosmetic fix. It immediately caught three tests in `tests/test_brain_failover.py` pinning the retired id as a resolver fixture; they now use a catalogued id plus a guard that fails plainly if it ever leaves the catalogue. Files: `config/models.yaml`, `config/llm/models.yaml`, `packages/ai/brain_config.py`, `tests/test_one_model_catalogue.py`, `tests/test_brain_failover.py`.
- **The agency implemented a change, passed its tests, pushed it — and then died re-logging in to `gh`** (2026-08-28). With the database, model discovery and stale-branch defects fixed, run 33148970679 finally worked: the agentic loop ran **34 minutes** (previous attempts died at 56–59 seconds), `Run pytest` passed, and `Commit and push` landed a real commit on `claude/context-issue-1350` — `agent/trend_watcher.py` plus 134 lines of new tests. Then `Create pull request` failed in under a second. Its first command asked the GitHub CLI to store a token while `GH_TOKEN` was already exported, which the CLI refuses outright ("The value of the GH_TOKEN environment variable is being used for authentication") and exits non-zero. The other seventeen steps in the workflow simply set `GH_TOKEN` and call `gh`, and all of them work; only this one re-authenticated, and only this one failed. Removed. 2 tests, verified failing first — one asserts no step re-authenticates, the other that the step still receives a credential, so the fix cannot be "fixed" by deleting the token too. Files: `.github/workflows/process-quick-note.yml`, `tests/test_process_quick_note_workflow.py`.
- **Model ids are discovered from NVIDIA instead of hardcoded** (2026-08-28). The shared list in `.github/scripts/nvidia_models.py` was "live-verified 2026-06-14"; by 2026-08-27 two of its three ids answered `410 Gone` (end-of-life 2026-05-12, 2026-06-11 and 2026-08-26), and the agency had stopped four separate times for this one reason. Every previous fix was another hand-edited id that would rot on NVIDIA's next retirement. NVIDIA's own documentation says to find the model id by querying the models endpoint, so `live_model_ids()` now reads `GET /v1/models` and `rank_models()` orders the result Nemotron-first (then instruct/coder), dropping families that cannot drive a tool-calling loop at all — embedding, rerank, OCR, guard/safety, vision. `resolve_model_ids()` memoises the lookup, because the agent asks once a turn and runs up to 120 turns. Discovery never raises, but it is never silent either: a missing key, an unreachable provider or an unreadable response is logged with its reason, and falling back to the static floor says so explicitly — a run on the floor that looked identical to a healthy one would be the very pathology this module was written to end. Any failure degrades to a static floor, which is now deliberately one entry rather than padded with ids nobody has verified. All three agent scripts consume it. **No id in this repo has to be correct for the agency to find a working model** — which is the property that was missing every previous time. 13 tests in `tests/test_nvidia_model_discovery.py`, verified failing first, plus a regression check that removing the Nemotron preference or the non-chat filter breaks them. `tests/test_quick_note_engine.py` no longer greps that file for the literal "nemotron-super" — an assertion that inspected a module nothing imported and kept passing while the id it named went end-of-life; it now exercises the ranking. Files: `.github/scripts/nvidia_models.py`, `.github/scripts/implement_agent.py`, `.github/scripts/review_agent.py`, `.github/scripts/apply_review.py`, `tests/test_nvidia_model_discovery.py`, `tests/test_quick_note_engine.py`.
- **The implementer ran a four-day-old copy of itself, so fixing it changed nothing** (2026-08-28). `process-quick-note.yml` reuses an existing context branch with `git checkout -b "$CONTEXT_BRANCH" "origin/$CONTEXT_BRANCH"`, and every later step then executes the tree *on that branch* — including `.github/scripts/implement_agent.py` itself. Issue #1347's context branch was cut on 2026-08-24, so run 33146384139 on 2026-08-28 executed the pre-fix implementer: its log still contained the line "Using NVIDIA NIM as the primary engine", which master had deleted an hour earlier, and it walked the six retired model ids master no longer contained. The routing fix had landed and had no effect, and nothing said so — only comparing the run's log against master's source revealed it. The agent was also planning against product code four days stale, not just stale tooling. The branch step now merges `origin/master` before the agent runs, and on conflict starts a fresh branch from master rather than proceeding on stale code — which is what a human does before resuming work on an old branch. 3 tests, verified failing first. Files: `.github/workflows/process-quick-note.yml`, `tests/test_process_quick_note_workflow.py`.
- **Three autonomous agent scripts each carried their own dead NVIDIA model list** (2026-08-27). `.github/scripts/nvidia_models.py` opens by calling itself "the single source of truth for all autonomous agent scripts", but nothing imported it: `implement_agent.py` held six ids, `review_agent.py` five, and `apply_review.py` four (one of them listed twice under two different labels, spending one of its four attempts re-asking a model that had just refused). The three lists overlapped but were not equal, and by 2026-08-27 every id in all three was retired — so routing the implementer through `ProviderRouter` left the council-review and apply-review steps, which run later in the *same* workflow, still pointed at models NVIDIA had already withdrawn. That is how the outage survived its own first fix. Both review scripts now import the shared list, leaving one place to correct. Their provider posture is deliberately unchanged: both keep the Anthropic fallback they have always had, unlike `implement_agent.py`, where it was removed on purpose. This does not claim any curated id is live — that cannot be known without a provider key — only that there is now a single list to fix. 23 tests in `tests/test_agent_scripts_share_one_model_list.py`, verified failing first. One of them exists because the change introduced a `NameError`: `review_agent.py` was switched to import `NVIDIA_MODEL_IDS` while its loop still read `NVIDIA_CANDIDATE_MODELS`, which every source-text assertion happily passed and which would have crashed every council review. The AST guard that now catches it checks that every model-list name a script *reads* is one something *binds*. Files: `.github/scripts/review_agent.py`, `.github/scripts/apply_review.py`, `tests/test_agent_scripts_share_one_model_list.py`.
- **The implementer carried its own model list, every entry died, and the whole agency went quiet** (2026-08-27). `.github/scripts/implement_agent.py` held six hardcoded NVIDIA NIM model ids and hand-rolled its own failover, in defiance of rule 2. By 2026-08-27 every one was dead: four returned `410 Gone` (`nvidia/llama-3.3-nemotron-super-49b-v1.5` and `meta/llama-3.3-70b-instruct` retired at 09:00 on 2026-08-26, `qwen/qwen3-coder-480b-a35b-instruct` on 2026-06-11, `qwen/qwen2.5-coder-32b-instruct` on 2026-05-12) and `nvidia/llama-3.1-nemotron-ultra-253b-v1` returned `404`. The loop walked the whole list on turn 1, logged "All candidate models exhausted" and exited — 59 seconds of work, no diff, on every issue. Routing now goes through `packages/ai/router.py` (`ProviderRouter`), which already owns the Cerebras → Groq → NVIDIA → Ollama chain and rule 4's `410` dead-model tracking, so a retired model costs one request rather than the agency. The script names no model and no endpoint at all: `_candidate_models()` resolves each provider's own `default_model` and skips ids a prior `410` flagged. **The router is restricted to `openai-compatible` providers on purpose** — the `anthropic`, `bedrock` and ollama-native paths rewrite the payload through converters that carry no `tools` field, so a tool-calling turn sent to one would not error, it would silently lose every tool definition. Paid providers are excluded when the router is *built*, not only via `allow_commercial_fallback=False` at call time: `chat_completion` guards with `if not first_eligible and is_commercial_provider(...)`, so the first eligible provider is used whatever it costs, and a host whose only openai-compatible key is a paid one (OpenRouter, Zhipu, MiniMax, DeepSeek, Mistral) would have been billed on the first turn — the flag stays as the second line of defence. An XML-tool-call responder is dropped from the router and failed over rather than retried, because the router is priority-ordered and stateless between calls, so re-sending the same payload draws the identical malformed reply. A model *is* named when NVIDIA leads the chain, drawn from the repo's curated `.github/scripts/nvidia_models.py` (which until now nothing imported, despite calling itself the single source of truth): `ProviderRouter`'s NVIDIA entry carries a hardcoded `default_model` that is itself one of the models retired on 2026-08-26, so naming nothing would have left `_candidate_models()` with exactly one dead candidate and reproduced the turn-1 outage on an NVIDIA-only runner. Every other provider still resolves its own default. `temperature` is left unset so the tools-blind response cache (keyed on model/messages/temperature/max_tokens/stop, active only at `temperature == 0`) can never replay a stale tool call into a live loop. The startup guard that demanded `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `NVIDIA_API_KEY` specifically is gone — it would have refused to start on a host configured with Cerebras or Groq alone. 17 tests in `tests/test_implement_agent_routing.py`, all verified failing first. `tests/test_quick_note_engine.py::test_implement_agent_nvidia_primary` asserted the literal string "Using NVIDIA NIM as the primary engine" — it encoded the premise that turned out to be the bug, so it is replaced by two tests carrying its real intent: the loop never escalates to a paid provider (`allow_commercial_fallback=False`, a stronger guarantee than a comment ordering check, since the router decides what counts as commercial) and it routes through the shared router with no hardcoded endpoint. Files: `.github/scripts/implement_agent.py`, `tests/test_implement_agent_routing.py`, `tests/test_quick_note_engine.py`.
- **The autonomous implementer could never report success on any issue, and its failure looked like success** (2026-08-27). `process-quick-note.yml` ran `pytest` with no MongoDB service, while `ci.yml` — the PR gate — has declared one all along. `.github/scripts/implement_agent.py` refuses to accept `IMPLEMENTATION_COMPLETE` unless the last `pytest` exited 0 (its `last_pytest_passed` gate), so with `localhost:27017` refused, `tests/test_auth_me_regression.py` errors, `pytest -x` stops there, and that gate was unsatisfiable on *every* issue regardless of what the agent wrote. Nothing surfaced it, because the downstream steps are gated by `if:` conditions rather than failing: the agentic loop "succeeded", steps 9–14 skipped, the retry handler bumped the issue's counter, and the job exited 0. Observed in run 33056034069 on issue #1319 — 2m41s of work, retry:1 → retry:2, green tick. Nine issues accumulated behind a workflow whose Actions list showed nothing wrong. `tests/conftest.py` already names this defect and records the fix (the service, not a `STORAGE_BACKEND=sqlite` pin, which leaks a non-daemon aiosqlite thread and runs the job toward GitHub's 360-minute ceiling) — it was applied to `agency-cycle.yml` and `continuous-improvement.yml` and never reached the workflow that does the implementing. This adds the same `mongo:7` service and a final step that exits 1 when the retry handler fired, so a barren run is red in the Actions list instead of indistinguishable from one that shipped a PR. Both workflows also carry `ci.yml`'s explicit `Wait for MongoDB` step: the service health check already gates step start, but a pytest that begins a moment early would reproduce this defect silently, and the log line is what makes the difference visible next time. The step runs last and is gated on the retry handler's own outcome, so label bookkeeping still completes and idle ticks with no issue to pick up stay green. Files: `.github/workflows/process-quick-note.yml`, `tests/test_process_quick_note_workflow.py`.
- **A conflicted Dependabot PR fell through to the arming path and stuck there forever** (2026-08-26). The sweep branched on `UNKNOWN` and `BEHIND` but not `DIRTY`, so a PR with a merge conflict reached the up-to-date path: classified, auto-merge armed, and never revisited — a conflicted PR is not `BEHIND`, so nothing brings it back. Auto-merge on a conflict waits indefinitely. Observed live in sweep run 32977520893 (13:59): #1335 read `DIRTY`, classified `patch`, and was armed; the log line said `update type: patch` and nothing else, indistinguishable from a healthy arm. This gets worse as the backlog drains rather than better — every dependency bump edits `requirements.txt`, so each merge makes a conflict likelier for whatever is still queued. The sweep now handles `DIRTY` explicitly: it asks Dependabot to rebase, which is the only actor that can resolve it (the conflict is in a file Dependabot generates, and it rebases its own PRs on request), never arms a conflicted PR, and does not spend the one-update-per-run budget on it since `update-branch` cannot fix a conflict. The nudge is skipped when the newest comment already carries it, so an hourly sweep does not turn a standing request into 24 comments a day. Files: `.github/workflows/dependabot-auto-merge.yml`, `tests/test_dependabot_sweep_workflow.py`.
- **The classifier read only the newest commit, so every branch the sweep updated became unclassifiable** (2026-08-26). `gh pr update-branch` appends a `Merge branch 'master' into …` commit, which carries no `compare/vOLD...vNEW` link. Reading `commits[-1]` therefore returned `unknown` for any PR the sweep had itself updated — and `unknown` is deliberately not auto-mergeable, so the recovery path could never arm anything it touched. Reproduced against PR #1342 (`boto3 1.43.71 → 1.43.77`, a patch): three `update-branch` passes buried the Dependabot commit under merge commits and the verdict came back `unknown`. Worse, it was silent in the way this whole class of bug has been all session — the log line reads `update type: unknown … leaving it for human review`, indistinguishable from a deliberate safety decision. It had not yet caused a stall only because all 10 remaining PRs still carried auto-merge armed by the pre-classifier blanket pass. `classify_pull_request()` now scans every commit rather than the newest; `#1342` reads `patch`, `#1336` still reads `major`, and a PR whose Dependabot commit is genuinely absent still reads `unknown` rather than fabricating a verdict. Files: `scripts/classify_dependabot_update.py`, `tests/test_classify_dependabot_update.py`.
- **The sweep drained the backlog slower than Dependabot refilled it** (2026-08-26). Branch protection requires an up-to-date branch, so merging one PR puts every other open one back to `BEHIND` — observed live: #1346 merged at 06:14 and #1345 read `mergeable_state: behind` immediately after, its base pinned to the commit #1346 had just superseded. The backlog therefore drains at exactly **one PR per sweep run**, however many branches a run refreshes. At the previous daily cadence that is 14 days to clear a batch Dependabot refills weekly, so it could never catch up. The sweep now runs hourly and updates **at most one** stale branch per run: refreshing the rest fires two CI runs each (`push` plus `pull_request`) and throws away all but one result, so bounding it drains at the same rate for a fraction of the cost. The bound self-advances — a branch that comes back red simply stops being `BEHIND`, so the next run moves to the next PR rather than stalling. PRs that are already up to date are still classified and armed every run, which costs no CI. `loops/registry.yaml` updated to `runs_per_day: 24` (rule 35). Files: `.github/workflows/dependabot-auto-merge.yml`, `loops/registry.yaml`, `tests/test_dependabot_sweep_workflow.py`.
- **The sweep was about to auto-merge a breaking dependency upgrade, because the job that classifies them skips once the sweep touches a branch** (2026-08-26). The recovery sweep deferred major-bump detection to the event-driven `auto-merge` job, which is guarded by `github.actor == 'dependabot[bot]'`. Authenticating the sweep with `GH_PAT` makes the actor a real user, so that job skips — confirmed on PR #1336, run 32936415712, actor `strikersam`, conclusion `skipped`. The marker comment it was supposed to write therefore never appeared, and the next sweep pass would have armed auto-merge on `anthropic >=0.122.0 → >=1.0.0` with nothing left in the loop to object — precisely what rule 40 reserves for a human. `scripts/classify_dependabot_update.py` gives the sweep a verdict it owns: it reads the `compare/vOLD...vNEW` link Dependabot writes into every commit message (which beats parsing pip range syntax such as `from <6,>=5.0.0 to >=5.0.1,<6`), and treats a grouped PR as minor/patch by construction because `.github/dependabot.yml` declares those groups `update-types: ["minor", "patch"]`. Auto-merge is armed only for `group`/`minor`/`patch`; `major` and `unknown` are left for review and commented once. `unknown` is deliberately handled like `major` — #1346 (reportlab) carries no compare link because reportlab.com is not GitHub, and a verdict the classifier could not reach is not one to merge unattended. Below 1.0.0 a minor bump is also counted as major, since 0.x is allowed to break. 30 tests, every case taken verbatim from the 14 stranded PRs. Files: `scripts/classify_dependabot_update.py`, `.github/workflows/dependabot-auto-merge.yml`, `tests/test_classify_dependabot_update.py`, `tests/test_dependabot_sweep_workflow.py`.
- **Updating those stale branches with `GITHUB_TOKEN` traded green checks for checks awaiting a human click** (2026-08-26). Verifying the previous fix instead of trusting its exit code turned up a third gate behind the first two. `gh pr update-branch` did move 10 of the 14 stranded Dependabot PRs onto current `master`, but the merge commits it created were attributed to `github-actions[bot]`, and GitHub does not run workflows for those automatically: all 10 resulting runs came back `action_required`. PR #1345 shows the before/after plainly — CI run 32696410024 passed on the old head, run 32934966004 on the updated head is `action_required` — so the update replaced a green required check with a pending one and the PRs were no closer to merging. The sweep now authenticates with `secrets.GH_PAT`, which is available to it precisely because it runs on schedule/dispatch rather than on the `dependabot[bot]` event that withholds repository secrets from the other job; that job must keep using `GITHUB_TOKEN`, so the two tokens differ on purpose and a test pins the asymmetry. Two further silent no-ops go with it: a `mergeStateStatus` of `UNKNOWN` (GitHub computes mergeability lazily, and 4 of the 14 answered `UNKNOWN` on the first poll and were skipped without a word) is now re-polled and, if still unresolved, warned about; and the sweep no longer arms auto-merge on a PR it cannot classify. Only the event-driven job has the `pull_request` payload `fetch-metadata` needs, so the sweep updates stale branches and lets the resulting `synchronize` event rule on them, and refuses to arm any PR that job has flagged as a major bump — which keeps rule 40 (a dependency upgrade with a breaking change needs a human) true for `anthropic >=0.122.0 → >=1.0.0` in #1336 and `reportlab` in #1346. Files: `.github/workflows/dependabot-auto-merge.yml`, `tests/test_dependabot_sweep_workflow.py`.
- **Enabling auto-merge was not enough: every stranded Dependabot PR was `BEHIND` its base and would have sat there forever** (2026-08-26). The sweep added earlier enabled GitHub auto-merge on all 14 stranded PRs without a single warning, and nothing merged — because branch protection refuses an out-of-date branch. Each PR's base was still pinned to a commit from 2026-08-23 while master had moved on, so `mergeable_state` read `behind` and auto-merge stayed armed but idle. The sweep looked like it had worked while changing nothing, which is the same class of silent no-op it was written to fix. It now checks `mergeStateStatus` per PR and, on `BEHIND`, updates the branch (falling back to an `@dependabot rebase` comment), which re-runs CI and lets auto-merge complete on its own. Dependabot would have rebased these itself eventually, but only on its own schedule — which is precisely what let them rot for days. Files: `.github/workflows/dependabot-auto-merge.yml`.
- **`quick-note:exhausted` was a counter, not a judgement — so unfixable tasks were blamed on the agent and buried** (2026-08-26). `process-quick-note.yml` marked an issue exhausted purely on three failed attempts: add the label, paste 40 lines of log, walk away. Nothing ever asked *why*, so "the agent could not write the fix" and "the agent was handed a task that was never fixable" were treated identically. The second is what kept happening — a parser bug fed the loop `qwen-proxy:app_settings.py:70`, a captured log record rather than a test, so every `pytest` on it died with `file or directory not found`, burned the retry budget and abandoned the issue. 238 closed issues carry that shape, and nine draft plan PRs were left stranded behind them. The diagnosis was always available and ignored: the repo's own `SelfHealingAgent._classify_failure()` correctly reported the MongoDB outage in #1360 as `infrastructure_error — "no code change can fix it"`, and the workflow marked it exhausted anyway. New `scripts/analyze_exhaustion.py` now gates the label behind a verdict, reusing that classifier and `parse_pytest_failures.py`: `needs-triage` when no real node ID can be recovered (the input is the defect), `blocked-infrastructure` when every failure is an unavailable service (no diff could fix it), and `exhausted` only for real, collectable tests that genuinely resisted the fix. The first two keep the issue open and retryable and record why; only the third spends the label. 11 tests. Files: `scripts/analyze_exhaustion.py`, `.github/workflows/process-quick-note.yml`, `tests/test_analyze_exhaustion.py`.
- **Draft context PRs could become unreachable to every workflow, with nothing to say so** (2026-08-26). `issue-context-generator.yml` opens a draft PR carrying a plan and `process-quick-note.yml` implements it — but the implementer only looks at *open* issues that are not `quick-note:exhausted` / `quick-note:rejected`. The moment an issue closed or exhausted, its draft PR was orphaned: no workflow would ever look at it again. Nine had accumulated (#1318, #1323, #1327, #1332, #1348, #1351, #1353, #1357, #1359), every one of their issues closed and exhausted. New `orphaned-pr-sweep.yml` (daily 06:00 UTC, plus a `dry_run` dispatch) is the missing return path. It deliberately does **not** strip `quick-note:exhausted` wherever it finds it — that would undo the analysis gate above and send the loop back to grinding on work already judged unfixable. `scripts/triage_orphaned_context_prs.py` holds the decision and is unit-tested: reopen a closed issue whose plan PR is still open; clear an exhaustion carrying no recorded analysis (labelled by the old bare counter, so never actually judged); leave alone an exhaustion that *does* carry one, and never touch `quick-note:rejected`, which is a deliberate decision not to build rather than a failure to. 9 tests; `loops/registry.yaml` updated (rule 35), audit reports `Drift: none`. Files: `.github/workflows/orphaned-pr-sweep.yml`, `scripts/triage_orphaned_context_prs.py`, `tests/test_triage_orphaned_context_prs.py`, `loops/registry.yaml`.
- **Dependabot auto-merge had never worked once: `secrets.GH_PAT` is always empty on Dependabot events** (2026-08-26). GitHub withholds repository secrets from workflows triggered by `dependabot[bot]`, so `GH_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GH_PAT }}` expanded to an empty string and every run died with `gh: To use GitHub CLI in a GitHub Actions workflow, set the GH_TOKEN environment variable` (exit 4). The tell was in the same job: the `dependabot/fetch-metadata` step succeeded on the identical event because it uses `secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN` — the special Actions token, not a repo secret. Both the merge step and the "flag major bump" comment step failed on every PR, which is why 13 Dependabot PRs sat fully green and unmerged. Both now use `secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN`, and `--admin` becomes `--auto`: `GITHUB_TOKEN` cannot bypass branch protection, and letting GitHub merge once required checks pass is the safer behaviour anyway (a direct squash is kept as a fallback for repos with "Allow auto-merge" switched off). Files: `.github/workflows/dependabot-auto-merge.yml`.
- **Stranded Dependabot PRs now recover instead of parking forever** (2026-08-26). The workflow's only trigger was `pull_request`, which fires once per PR — so the failure above stranded each PR with nothing to retry it, and the backlog could only grow. A `sweep-stranded` job now runs daily at 07:00 UTC (and on `workflow_dispatch`) and enables auto-merge on every open Dependabot PR. It deliberately does not filter on update type — `fetch-metadata` needs a PR event payload a scheduled run does not have — so it enables auto-merge and lets required checks decide: a red, conflicted, or branch-protected PR simply stays open, and the sweep can never force one through. Per-PR failures are non-fatal so one unmergeable PR cannot abort the rest. `loops/registry.yaml` updated to match (`trigger: schedule`, daily cadence); `loop_registry.py audit --check` reports `Drift: none`. Files: `.github/workflows/dependabot-auto-merge.yml`, `loops/registry.yaml`.
- **The autonomous loop escalated to a human on every run because the failing-test parser matched log lines, not test IDs** (2026-08-25). Two workflows extracted failing node IDs from pytest output with a grep, and both were wrong in opposite directions. `agency-cycle.yml` used `grep -E '^(FAILED|ERROR) '`, which also matches pytest's captured **log records** — rendered as `LEVEL    logger:file.py:lineno message` — so `ERROR    qwen-proxy:app_settings.py:70 ...` was turned by `awk '{print $2}'` into the "failing test" `qwen-proxy:app_settings.py:70`. Every follow-up `pytest` on that string died with `file or directory not found`, so the self-healing agent could never converge and opened an escalation issue on every run (#1354, plus the `docs: investigate failing tests` draft PRs #1318/#1323/#1327/#1332/#1348/#1351/#1353 that never landed). `continuous-improvement.yml` used `grep '^FAILED '`, which never matches an `ERROR` summary line, so a run whose only failure was a fixture or collection error reported no failing tests at all (#1352). Both now call a shared, unit-tested `scripts/parse_pytest_failures.py`, which reads only pytest's `short test summary info` block and shape-checks every candidate — the file part must end in `.py` and contain no colon — so a `logger:file.py:lineno` locator can never be emitted as a node ID. Files: `scripts/parse_pytest_failures.py`, `.github/workflows/agency-cycle.yml`, `.github/workflows/continuous-improvement.yml`, `tests/test_pytest_failure_parser.py`.
- **The autonomous-loop workflows ran pytest with no database, so the self-healing agent saw a red suite the PR gate never did** (2026-08-26). `.github/workflows/ci.yml` declares a `mongo:7` service and `tests/conftest.py`'s own docstring documents the contract — "the CI job provides a real mongo:7 service… the `client` fixture connects to the real database". `agency-cycle.yml` and `continuous-improvement.yml` declared no service at all, and `db/__init__.py` resolves `STORAGE_BACKEND` to `"mongo"` by default, so every test reaching the real store (`/api/admin/seed` → `seed_admin()` behind the `backend_jwt` fixture) dialled `localhost:27017` and raised `ServerSelectionTimeoutError`. One error, in the two workflows nobody watches, filed daily as #1352/#1354 while `ci.yml` stayed green — which is exactly why it survived for weeks. Both workflows now declare the same service. **Do not "fix" this by pinning `STORAGE_BACKEND=sqlite` in conftest:** it clears the error and then hangs the suite, because routing everything through SQLiteStore leaves an unclosed aiosqlite connection whose `_connection_worker_thread` is non-daemon and waits on its queue forever with no atexit handler. Every test passes and the interpreter never exits — measured at 51+ minutes and still running against master's 3m27s for the same step, on a job that declares no `timeout-minutes` and so runs toward GitHub's 360-minute ceiling. conftest carries that reasoning inline, a test asserts the pin is not reintroduced, and a new `pytest_sessionfinish` hook prints any lingering non-daemon thread by name so the next occurrence is obvious rather than mysterious. Files: `.github/workflows/agency-cycle.yml`, `.github/workflows/continuous-improvement.yml`, `tests/conftest.py`, `tests/test_conftest_hermetic_env.py`.
- **Split admin identity in the test suite: `seed_admin()` seeded one address while tests logged in as another** (2026-08-25). Six test modules ran `os.environ.setdefault("ADMIN_EMAIL", "admin@test.local")` at *import* time. `backend/server.py` resolves `ADMIN_EMAIL` once at its own import — already done via conftest — so it kept seeding `admin@llmrelay.local`, while every test module imported *after* one of those six re-read the env and authenticated as `admin@test.local`. `POST /api/admin/seed` returned 200 and the very next `POST /api/auth/login` returned `401 Invalid credentials`, which read like a password bug; `tests/test_activity_feed.py` never even used the value it set. `tests/conftest.py` now pins `ADMIN_EMAIL` once (it imports before any test module, so a later `setdefault` is a no-op) and the six dead module-level assignments are deleted. This was latent for as long as the Mongo default masked it — the store-level `ServerSelectionTimeoutError` aborted the fixture before it ever reached login — so fixing the storage default alone would have swapped one red error for another. Files: `tests/conftest.py`, `tests/test_activity_feed.py`, `tests/test_backend_runtime_bootstrap.py`, `tests/test_backend_server_features.py`, `tests/test_bootstrap_source_id_index.py`, `tests/test_quick_note_to_task.py`, `tests/test_social_login_oauth.py`, `tests/test_conftest_hermetic_env.py`.
- **Voice memory-kernel tests accumulated state across runs, so a second local `pytest` went red** (2026-08-25). `voice/memory_kernel.py` binds its storage directory at *import* time (`_DATA_DIR = Path(os.environ.get("MEMORY_KERNEL_DIR", ".data/memory"))`), so the `tmp_kernel` fixture's `monkeypatch.setenv` only took effect when that fixture happened to trigger the module's first import. Running `tests/test_voice_pipeline.py` alone did exactly that and passed forever; under the full suite an earlier import won the race, the kernel bound to the repo-local `.data/memory`, and stored facts survived between runs — `test_memory_reinforcement` saw `reinforcement_count` 2 on a fresh checkout and 4 on the next run, with `confidence` drifting 1.0 → 1.1. CI never saw it because every run is a fresh clone, but the pre-push hook's `pytest -x` fails for any developer who runs the suite twice. `tests/conftest.py` now pins `MEMORY_KERNEL_DIR` to a per-session temp directory; conftest is imported before any test module, so it beats the import race regardless of ordering, and nothing is written into the working tree. Files: `tests/conftest.py`.
- **The "Autonomous Fix" CI-repair bot can no longer make destructive, unreviewed pushes** (2026-08-20). Within a few hours on PR #1285, the previous inline (ungated) version of `.github/workflows/autonomous-fix.yml` wrote an LLM's raw JSON output straight to disk with zero review: it invented two new duplicate/broken GitHub Actions workflow files from nothing, then — because it had been fed 3000 characters of MongoDB service-container log noise instead of the actual pytest failure — "fixed" a test by shrinking `tests/conftest.py` (the shared pytest bootstrap every test in the suite depends on) from 354 lines to 10. None of the three attempts touched the real bug. Root cause was a stub in a new test file that unconditionally replaced the real, already-imported `langfuse_obs.emit_chat_observation` with a `MagicMock()` at collection time with no teardown, corrupting `inspect.signature()` for every later test in the same pytest session; fixed by scoping the stub to only-when-not-already-imported (`tests/test_daily_automation_2026_08_16.py`).
  - Extracted the workflow's logic into `.github/scripts/autonomous_fix.py`, wired through the same slop-gate `autonomous_agent.py` already uses (`is_destructive_overwrite` / `looks_like_secret_file` / `python_parses` from `.github/scripts/slop_gate.py`) — the exact check that would have caught the conftest.py truncation.
  - New hard denylist: CI workflow files, `.github/scripts/` (the bot's own code), `tests/conftest.py`, and CLAUDE.md's risky-module list (`packages/auth/*`, `key_store.py`, `agent/tools.py`, `handlers/v3_auth.py`) can never be touched by this bot.
  - The bot may only **modify existing files**, never create new ones — a CI fix has no legitimate reason to invent a file that didn't exist before.
  - Real log extraction (`_extract_pytest_failure`): pulls the actual pytest `FAILURES` / `short test summary info` section out of the job log, stripping verbose service-container JSON noise first, instead of blindly truncating raw text.
  - Pre-commit verification: the proposed fix is run through `python -m compileall` and `pytest -x` locally before anything is committed; a fix that doesn't actually pass is discarded with a PR comment explaining why, never pushed.
  - A retry cap (2 attempts per PR): after that, the bot stops and asks for a human instead of compounding wrong fixes every 30 minutes indefinitely.
  - All-or-nothing: if any single proposed file is blocked, the whole attempt is declined — never a partially-applied fix.
  - Files: `.github/scripts/autonomous_fix.py` (new), `.github/workflows/autonomous-fix.yml`, `tests/test_autonomous_fix.py` (new). The `langfuse_obs` stub fix itself (`tests/test_daily_automation_2026_08_16.py`) landed separately, on PR #1285's own branch.

- **The github MCP row no longer reads as an error on Providers → MCP** (2026-08-20). The github server is a stdio MCP the long-lived backend structurally cannot dial, but its capability is served to agents by `agent/github_tools.py` (using `GH_PAT`), so it is genuinely usable. `_status_for` mapped every undialable server to the muted `unavailable`, which — like the amber `unconfigured` on unset servers — still reads as a problem an operator goes hunting for. A new `MCPServerSpec.served_by_backend` flag marks such servers, and they now report a green `available` state (with the reason explaining the backend serves them) instead. An undialable server with no alternate path still reports `unavailable`, and a dialable server that fails to answer is still a red `error` — the distinction does not swallow real faults. Frontend renders `available` with the same green treatment as `connected`. Files: `packages/integrations/mcp_registry.py`, `frontend/src/v5/screens/ProvidersScreen.jsx`, `tests/test_mcp_registry.py`.

- **Track `RENDER_WORKSPACE_ID` in `render.yaml` so the autonomous RenderOps loop survives IaC redeploys** (2026-08-15). RenderOps calls the Render MCP with an explicit `workspaceId` on every resource tool (upstream deprecated implicit session-scoped selection); with it unset, service discovery failed with "no workspace selected" and the self-healing loop stayed dark. The workspace (owner) id is an account identifier, not a secret, so it is now committed as a value rather than left `sync:false` (dashboard-only), keeping the ops loop configured across redeploys. File: `render.yaml`.

- **Autonomous tasks execute again: fast planner model, a "won't do" status for rejects, retry-all-blocked, and comment-driven re-runs** (2026-08-15). Diagnosed from the live Render backend, where `#1278`'s token-budget cap was deployed but every task still failed at `planning: TimeoutError` (~120s/attempt) or the outer `Execution timed out after 600s`.
  - **Root cause: the planner ran a reasoning model that can't answer in time on the free tier.** A startup migration force-set every role to `z-ai/glm-5.2`, and its model id pins planning to the slow NVIDIA free tier (fast Cerebras is never tried because it doesn't serve that id). The migration now moves the two latency-critical roles (planner + executor) off reasoning models (`z-ai/glm-5.*`, the nemotron reasoners, `deepseek-r1`) to a fast non-reasoning MoE (`meta/llama-4-maverick-17b-128e-instruct`), keeping the reasoning model for verifier/judge. Both a fresh reset and an in-place "fast-path the planner" branch are covered, so the existing prod config is migrated on the next deploy without disturbing a deliberate verify/judge choice. File: `backend/server.py`.
  - **Rejected tasks now go to a terminal `wont_do` status, not BLOCKED/FAILED.** A human declining work is a decision, not a system failure — routing it to BLOCKED made the auto-retry reconciler and the blocked-backlog retirement churn or mislabel it. New `TaskStatus.WONT_DO` (reopenable via Retry), the pre-execution reject path and the Telegram Reject button both target it, and it's a distinct board column. Files: `tasks/models.py`, `tasks/service.py`, `frontend/src/v5/screens/TaskBoardScreen.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/TasksPage.js`.
  - **Retry-all-blocked.** New `POST /api/tasks/retry-blocked` re-queues every BLOCKED task in one call (own tasks; admin also clears the shared `system` queue), with a "↻ Retry all blocked (N)" button on the board — so once the cause is fixed the blocked column clears in one click instead of card-by-card. Files: `tasks/api.py`, `frontend/src/api.js`, `frontend/src/v5/screens/TaskBoardScreen.jsx`.
  - **A comment is now an instruction.** A top-level human comment on a task of ANY status re-opens and re-queues it (the body is carried into the agent's conversation context via `follow_up`), so the agent reads and acts on it — including a requested status change. A threaded reply stays discussion and does not re-run. File: `tasks/api.py`.
  - Regression tests: reject→`wont_do` + reopen (`test_task_pre_execution_gate.py`, `test_telegram_task_callback.py`, `test_tasks_awaiting_approval_api.py`), retry-blocked bulk requeue, and comment-reengage vs. threaded-reply (`test_tasks_awaiting_approval_api.py`).

- **The `planning: TimeoutError` that stalled every autonomous task is fixed, and the planner model / budget / timeout are now tunable from the Brain card** (2026-08-15). The agent loop hardcoded `max_tokens=16384` for every LLM call and ignored the `BrainConfig.max_tokens` the UI already exposed. On the free tier the planner defaults to a heavy *reasoning* model (`nvidia/llama-3.3-nemotron-super-49b-v1`), which spent that whole budget on thinking tokens and blew the failover deadline — the `Runtime 'internal_agent' execution error: planning: TimeoutError` seen continuously in the Render logs, which then re-queued 5× and blocked. Fixes:
  - **`_chat_text` now resolves the output-token budget and per-request timeout from `BrainConfig`** (`resolve_agent_generation_sync`, cache-first with env fallback), replacing the 16384 hardcode with the configurable value (default 4096) and passing the timeout through to `failover_chat_completion`. Bounding the budget stops a reasoning model from running the planner past the deadline. Files: `agent/loop.py`, `packages/ai/brain_config.py`.
  - **`request_timeout_sec` added to `BrainConfig` + `BrainConfigPatch`** (default 120, 15–600), and the **Brain card** now renders **Max tokens** and **Timeout (s)** inputs alongside the existing per-role model pickers — so the operator can switch the planner to a fast non-reasoning model (e.g. `meta/llama-3.3-70b-instruct` or Cerebras `qwen-3-coder-480b`) and tune the budget/timeout with no redeploy. New env fallbacks `AGENT_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` / `AGENT_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_SEC` for cold-cache/headless installs. Files: `packages/ai/brain_config.py`, `frontend/src/v5/components/BrainCard.jsx`, `docs/configuration-reference.md`, `.env.example`.
  - Regression tests: `resolve_agent_generation_sync` precedence/clamping and the new patch field (`tests/test_brain_config_store.py`), plus a loop test asserting `_chat_text` sends the resolved `max_tokens` and timeout rather than 16384 (`tests/test_agent_runner.py`).
- **Production bugs from the external (MiniMax) code audit — eight fixed, two were false positives** (2026-08-15). Each reported issue was re-verified against the code; the real ones are fixed with regression tests in `tests/test_minimax_bug_fixes.py`.
  - **JWT signing secret was regenerated on every restart.** `JWT_SECRET` fell back to `secrets.token_hex(32)` when unset, minting a fresh key each process start and invalidating every session/token on the routine Render cold-start. It now derives a *stable* key from the (required, restart-stable) `ADMIN_PASSWORD` when `JWT_SECRET` is unset — with a warning to set it explicitly — and only uses an ephemeral key under `TESTING`. File: `backend/server.py`.
  - **Scheduler dispatch used the deprecated `asyncio.get_event_loop()` and ran on the wrong loop.** APScheduler's `BackgroundScheduler` fires `on_fire` from a worker thread, where the old sync callback created a throwaway loop and ran the orchestrator on it — a different loop from the one the Motor/aiosqlite clients are bound to, hanging on the first DB await. `_scheduler_on_fire` is now a coroutine, so `AgentScheduler` dispatches it onto the attached FastAPI main loop via `run_coroutine_threadsafe` (the already-tested path). Fixes both the deprecation warning and the cross-loop dispatch deadlock (two of the reported items share this root cause). File: `backend/server.py`.
  - **In-memory chat-session fallback grew without bound.** The limited-mode (`_LIMITED_CHAT_SESSIONS`) store used when MongoDB is unavailable is now an LRU capped at `LIMITED_CHAT_SESSIONS_MAX` (default 500), evicting least-recently-used sessions. File: `backend/server.py`.
  - **Container detection missed Podman/OCI.** Ollama's localhost→service-name rewrite only checked `/.dockerenv`, so under Podman/containerd/Kubernetes it never fired. New `_in_container()` also checks `/run/.containerenv`, the `container`/`KUBERNETES_SERVICE_HOST`/`IN_DOCKER` env vars, and cgroup markers. File: `backend/server.py`.
  - **The governance audit summary conflated all-time and windowed data.** `total_cost_usd`/`total_tokens`/`would_block`/`by_agent` were summed over the bounded ring buffer, so they silently undercounted once it wrapped. They are now true all-time accumulators updated at `record()` time, alongside a new `total_recorded` count and the windowed `buffered_events`. File: `packages/governance/audit.py`.
  - **Silent exception swallowing and prod-invisible fallback logs in the agent loop.** Three bare `except Exception: pass` sites in the planner path (learning-loop persistence, lessons/microagents injection) now log; and the "configured premium provider degraded to Ollama" fallbacks were promoted from `log.debug` (dropped at production's INFO threshold) to `log.warning` so a dead Opus/Bedrock key is visible. File: `agent/loop.py`.
  - **False positive — frontend token-refresh "race".** `frontend/src/api.js` already implements the correct single-flight + queue pattern (one refresh in flight, concurrent 401s queued and replayed with the new token), and `/api/auth/refresh` is stateless (no refresh-token rotation to mishandle). No code change; documented for traceability.
  - **False positive — "missing" OAuth CSRF state validation.** All four OAuth callbacks (GitHub login, GitHub repo-connect, Google login, GitHub-app repo-access) validate an unguessable server-side/session state and consume it to prevent replay. No code change; documented for traceability.
- **Cost tracker now prices `claude-opus-5`, `claude-opus-4-7`, `claude-opus-4-6`, and `claude-haiku-4-5`** (2026-08-15). All four models were referenced in `packages/ai/brain_config.py` — `claude-opus-5` as the planner and judge in the `anthropic` and `aerolink` presets, and `claude-opus-4-7`/`4-6` in the preferred-model list — but were absent from `_DEFAULT_COST_TABLE`. Every call to those models was tracked at $0, hiding real spend from the operator dashboard. Added with the correct pricing tier (opus: $15/$75 per million tokens; haiku-4-5 short alias: $0.8/$4.0 — matching the versioned `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` already present). 7 new regression tests pin each model's input and output cost, and verify that the opus 4.x versioned models all land in the same tier as `claude-opus-4-8`. Files: `packages/ai/cost_tracker.py`, `tests/test_cost_attribution.py`.

- **Scheduler run-once cleanup is now race-safe, and its removal branches are consolidated** (2026-08-15, #1207 / #1208). `force_cleanup()`'s two unfired-run-once expiry paths read a snapshot where `run_count == 0` and then deleted, but a concurrent `_fire()` (APScheduler fires on a background thread) can increment `run_count` in between — so a one-shot that had just begun firing could be deleted mid-flight and miscounted as expired. New `ScheduleStore.delete_if_unfired_run_once()` re-checks the condition atomically at delete time (via the delete filter on Mongo; under the same `self._lock` that `upsert` takes on SQLite and in memory), and `_expire_run_once_orphan()` prefers it so a firing row is left for the already-fired rule instead. Stores predating the method fall back to the prior unconditional remove. The four inline removal branches (7-day expiry, already-fired, stuck-agency, name-dedup) were consolidated behind shared `_remove_job()` / `_expire_run_once_orphan()` helpers, so a failed durable delete is no longer miscounted as a removal on **any** branch (previously only the every-minute orphan path had that guarantee) and each now deregisters the job from APScheduler. Files: `agent/schedule_store.py`, `packages/scheduler/scheduler.py`, `tests/test_force_cleanup_conditional_delete.py`.
- **Telegram Approve/Reject now fires for outward-facing autonomous work, and the dead-task backlog is auto-purged** (2026-08-14). Follow-up to the Task Board fixes, closing the two behaviours the operator asked for.
  - **The pre-execution approval gate never fired for autonomous tasks.** The gate (which parks a task and sends a Telegram Approve/Reject inline keyboard before the agent runs) keys off `requires_approval`, which autonomous CEO tasks never set — so the operator only ever saw the merge alert, never an approve/decline prompt. The dispatcher now promotes outward-facing tasks (task_types that commit + open a PR / deploy / release, or tasks tagged `deploy`/`pr-opened`/`external-write`/`risk:*`) to `requires_approval` on first dispatch, so they park at the existing gate and send the prompt. Internal work (research, self-heal, scheduling) is not in the outward set and stays fully autonomous. Gated by `AGENCY_GATE_OUTWARD_FACING` (default on); the classifier is total and fails open to autonomy on an unknown type, so a misclassification can never silently gate everything. Files: `tasks/service.py`, `tests/test_task_pre_execution_gate.py`.
  - **Dead terminal tasks accumulated forever.** A new self-heal pass deletes DONE/FAILED tasks older than `SELF_HEAL_PURGE_DAYS` (default 3), keeping the board tidy as the retirement pass ages blocked corpses into FAILED. Conservative — only terminal statuses, nothing newer than the cutoff, off-switch via `SELF_HEAL_PURGE_ENABLED` — and it invalidates the list caches so the freed board shows immediately. Files: `services/self_heal.py`, `tests/test_self_heal_v2.py`.

- **The Task Board no longer times out, and chronically-blocked tasks stop piling up** (2026-08-14). Two problems reported together ("tasks failing, too many blocked tasks, possible duplicates; the tasks screen takes an eternity to load or times out"), both diagnosed from the live Render backend where the board sat at ~162 tasks.
  - **The list endpoint fetched every task's full `execution_log` only to throw it away.** `GET /api/tasks` already dropped `execution_log` from the *response* (it can be 10k+ entries / 7MB+ on a long-lived autonomous task), but `TaskStore.list_all`/`list_for_user` still pulled the whole document off Mongo and deserialized it into `Task` models before the endpoint discarded the field — so the ~27s load / timeout was paid on the fetch, not the response. Both queries take `include_log=False` now, which projects the unbounded append-only fields (`execution_log`, `workflow_history`) out at the query so they are never fetched; the list endpoints pass it. Files: `tasks/store.py`, `tasks/api.py`, `tests/test_tasks.py`.
  - **Blocked tasks were never retired, so the free-tier restart loop grew the board without bound.** A task that exhausts its dispatch-retry budget goes BLOCKED; on the free tier the dominant reason is `planning: TimeoutError` on an auto-generated CEO task that will never plan, and the CEO re-issues it as a fresh `run_once` job on every OOM restart, so blocked corpses accumulate and keep churning the single dispatch worker. A new self-heal pass (`_heal_blocked_backlog`) retires tasks that have sat BLOCKED past `BLOCKED_TASK_RETIRE_SEC` (default 6h) to terminal FAILED, pinning `auto_retry_count` at the reconciler's cap so the FAILED pass cannot resurrect them. It scans light (no logs) and re-fetches each retiree in full before the whole-document `replace_one`, so the execution_log is preserved. Non-destructive — FAILED is reopenable by a human. Files: `services/self_heal.py`, `tests/test_self_heal_v2.py`.

- **Mitigate the RSS creep that was OOM-restarting the 512MB instance** (2026-08-14). Render memory metrics showed a textbook sawtooth: ~450MB baseline climbing ~10MB every 10 minutes to ~510MB (95% of the 512MB limit), then a restart back to ~130MB, on repeat — the intermittent crashes reported in production. CPU was idle throughout (~0.015 cores), so this was pure RSS growth, not load. Root cause: the service runs many threads for an async workload (motor, httpx, the APScheduler pool, every `asyncio.to_thread`) and glibc gives each thread its own malloc arena — up to 8×CPU — each retaining freed memory that the default allocator only ever returns to the OS from the *top* of the main heap. Freed blocks stranded mid-arena stay mapped, so RSS rises monotonically while live memory is flat. Two-part fix, both fail-soft and default-on: (1) `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2` + `MALLOC_TRIM_THRESHOLD_=100000` (set in `Dockerfile.backend` and `render.yaml`) cap how many arenas exist and lower the auto-trim threshold, holding the baseline down; (2) a new in-process memory guard (`services/memory_guard.py`, wired into `services/background.py`) runs `gc.collect()` + glibc `malloc_trim(0)` every 180s, which walks every arena and reclaims the mid-arena fragmentation the threshold cannot. Tunable via `MEMORY_GUARD_ENABLED` / `MEMORY_GUARD_INTERVAL_SEC` (interval floored to 30s so a bad value can't busy-loop); the trim is skipped on a non-glibc libc. Standard remedy for the pattern; the actual RSS curve is being confirmed against production after deploy. Files: `services/memory_guard.py`, `services/background.py`, `Dockerfile.backend`, `render.yaml`, `docs/configuration-reference.md`, `.env.example`, `tests/test_memory_guard.py`.

- **Portfolio (and all) tasks now surface by priority, and the Anthropic 400 that stalled the queue is fixed** (2026-08-14). Diagnosed from the live Render backend after "tons of portfolio tasks pending, not getting picked up". The tasks were eligible (`pending_agent_run=True`), but two things starved them:
  - **The dispatch queue was ordered oldest-updated-first with no priority tiebreak** (`TaskStore.list_pending`). Since the dispatcher pulls the front of that list (often `limit=1`), a freshly materialized HIGH-priority initiative queued behind the entire older backlog and was serviced last. `list_pending` now ranks by priority (urgent > high > medium > low) then oldest-updated within a tier — via a Mongo aggregation server-side, mirrored in the in-memory path. New `_priority_rank` helper is total (unknown/None → LOW). Files: `tasks/store.py`, `tests/test_list_pending_priority.py`.
  - **`anthropic-claude` returned `400 Bad Request` every cycle**, forcing 3-failure failover to NVIDIA and burning dispatch slots. Two spec violations in the request: `system` was sent as an explicit `null` (Anthropic requires a string or omission), and `max_tokens` was hardcoded to `16384`, which exceeds Claude 3.5 Sonnet's 8192 output cap (a 400 on that model). The `system` key is now included only when non-empty; `max_tokens` defaults to a safe 8192 and is tunable via `ANTHROPIC_MAX_TOKENS`; and a 4xx from Anthropic now logs the response body (previously discarded by `raise_for_status`, which is why the cause was invisible). File: `agent/loop.py`.
- **A planning timeout no longer surfaces as an undiagnosable `planning: `** (2026-08-14). Confirmed still firing on the live Render backend: the autonomous agency dispatches work 24×7, but tasks were failing with `Runtime 'internal_agent' execution error: planning: ` — an empty cause — then re-queuing 5/5 and never completing. The cause is `asyncio.TimeoutError()`, whose `str()` is the empty string, so the planner-phase error carried no information into the runtime error, the task's `error_message`, or any incident filed from it. `AgentRunner._plan` now falls back to the exception's class name when the underlying error has no message (`planning: TimeoutError`), making the single most common autonomous-loop failure legible without changing any control flow. File: `agent/loop.py`.
- **Four production bugs found by reading the live Render backend over MCP** (2026-08-14). The Render deploy is healthy, but its runtime logs showed four distinct recurring failures, each fixed here with a regression test:
  - **Every autonomy background cycle crashed with `name 'time' is not defined`.** `backend/server.py` imports the stdlib time module as `import time as _time`, but `_autonomy_bg_cycle` ended with a bare `time.time()` — a `LOAD_GLOBAL` for a name the module never binds, so the whole cycle raised on its last line every tick. Fixed to `_time.time()`. A structural regression test disassembles the function and asserts every global it loads resolves, catching this and any future same-shape typo. File: `backend/server.py`.
  - **Every task routed through the Hermes runtime failed 100% of the time.** The in-process Hermes server (`:8100`) reaches `InternalAgentAdapter` over an HTTP hop, and the orchestrator `_BYPASS` ContextVar a sanctioned caller sets does not cross that boundary, so `AgentRunner.run()` hit its orchestrator-mode block and raised "blocked in orchestrator mode". The Hermes server — itself a sanctioned autonomous execution endpoint — now sets the bypass locally around `execute()` and resets it in `finally`, mirroring `tasks/service.py`, `ceo_dispatcher`, and `direct_chat`.
